## PACKAGE A

FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

DELIVERABLE
One static poster, 4:5 aspect ratio (1080×1350 px minimum working canvas, scalable), print/digital-ready, single final composite. No alternate versions.

OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
Aight is a rupee-native AI infrastructure/API reseller for Indian businesses (per getaight.ai: prepaid wallet, hard caps, one domestic GST invoice, curated text/voice/image models, founder-level support). The festive promotion must state two exact, easy-to-read prices (₹9/image, ₹99/video) as the single commercial hook, but the poster must read as enterprise infrastructure collateral — closer to a rate card or invoice than a sale banner. The customer's real risk is tonal: festive + ultra-low price naturally pulls toward discount-retail visual language (starbursts, "SALE," strikethroughs, confetti). The brief explicitly forbids that outcome, so the creative solution must make the prices feel like precision-engineered unit economics, not a markdown.

CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
"The Festive Rate Card." Treat the poster as if Aight printed its own official price list for the festive season — the same artifact language the brand already uses on its site (counter, ledger, invoice, wallet, hard caps). The two prices are presented as two ledger line-items in a quiet, tabular, invoice-grade layout, not as promo tags. Festivity is expressed only through material and light (warm brass/gold on deep charcoal, a single restrained diya-glow and fine jali/rangoli hairline border) — never through discount iconography. Typography (weight, scale, tabular numerals, rule lines) is the entire hierarchy engine.

MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
Reading order, top to bottom, strictly grid-governed (each information kind gets its own fixed column/row band so ordering is signalled by geometry, not decoration):

1. Brand band (smallest, top): wordmark "aight." + tagline "Where Indian businesses buy AI."
2. Occasion line (quiet, small caps, gold): "Festive Season Rate Card" — states occasion without shouting sale.
3. HERO PRICE MODULE (dominant, centre, ~55% of vertical space): two stacked ledger rows separated by a thin gold hairline rule —
   Row A: "IMAGE GENERATION" (small label, letterspaced) → "₹9" (huge, tabular numerals) → "per generation" (small, beneath)
   Row B: "VIDEO GENERATION" (small label, letterspaced) → "₹99" (huge, tabular numerals) → "per generation" (small, beneath)
   ₹9 and ₹99 are set at identical cap-height so both register as equally serious, not as "small price / big price" joke pairing.
4. Credibility strip (small, monospace, one line): "Prepaid rupee wallet · One domestic GST invoice · GPT · Claude · Gemini · curated image & video models"
5. CTA band (bottom, quiet): "Talk to us — getaight.ai"

Per Samara's grid logic used here: like information (both prices) is grouped into one visually identical module so they're read as a matched pair; unlike information (brand, occasion, credibility, CTA) is separated into its own band with its own type size, so the eye path is unambiguous without needing arrows, badges or colour noise. In a visually busy/festive field, the size/weight contrast on the two price numerals is pushed further than usual (near-maximum type scale) precisely because everything else on the page is deliberately quiet — this is the single loud gesture the page is allowed.

VISUAL_SYSTEM
- Palette: near-black charcoal/ink base (#14140F–#1A1A14 range), warm brass/gold (#C9A063–#D4AF6A range) for numerals, rules and hairline motifs, warm off-white/parchment (#EDE6D6) for supporting text. No red/green/discount-tag colours anywhere.
- Typography: a serious grotesk or humanist sans for all labels/tagline/CTA (measured, not display-decorative); tabular/monospaced numerals specifically for ₹9 and ₹99 so they read like metered infrastructure pricing (ledger/terminal feel) rather than retail signage. Generous negative space around the price module — space itself signals premium.
- Motif: one fine gold hairline geometric border (rangoli/jali-inspired, abstracted to straight/radial line work, not floral clipart) confined to the outer 5–8% frame margin; one small, soft diya-glow light pool low in the frame (warm bokeh, no literal diya illustration) as the only "festive" light cue.
- Explicitly excluded: starbursts, ribbons, "SALE"/"OFFER" badges, strikethrough pricing, confetti, marigolds, fireworks, gradients that read as app-store or e-commerce banner style.
- Rule of thumb applied: when in doubt, remove an element rather than add one — the page should feel closer to a bank/API pricing page than a shop flyer.

PRODUCTION_RECIPE
Two-layer construction, because price accuracy is non-negotiable and generative text rendering is unreliable at this precision:
1. Generate a text-free background/atmosphere layer: dark charcoal canvas, fine gold hairline geometric border motif, single warm diya-glow light pool bottom-third, subtle premium paper/ink micro-texture, 4:5 framing, ample dark negative space at centre and top for type overlay.
2. Compose all typography, the wordmark, both prices, rules, credibility strip and CTA deterministically in a layout/vector tool, using the exact grid and type spec above, locked to the hex palette. Nothing textual is left to the generative pass.
3. Final export at 4:5, checked at thumbnail size to confirm ₹9 and ₹99 remain instantly legible even when scaled down (mobile feed size test).

GENERATION_PROMPTS
Single image-generation prompt (background/atmosphere layer only — no text, no numerals, no logos to be rendered by the model):

"A premium, minimal dark charcoal-black background for a 4:5 vertical infrastructure/fintech-style poster. Deep ink-black canvas (#14140F) with a fine hairline geometric border motif in warm brass-gold, inspired by abstracted rangoli/jali line patterns, confined to the outer edge only — no floral or figurative ornament. A single soft warm amber-gold glow pool of light, like a diya's light, positioned low and slightly off-centre, casting gentle bokeh upward, subtle enough not to overpower a large empty central and upper area reserved for typography. Extremely subtle fine paper-grain or brushed-metal micro-texture across the black field for material depth. No text, no numbers, no logos, no people, no product renders, no confetti, no starbursts, no discount badges. Mood: serious enterprise technology brand quietly acknowledging a festive season — restrained, dark, metallic-gold accent, high production value, ample negative space, symmetrical composition, vertical 4:5 format."

Construction plan overlay (deterministic, specified above in PRODUCTION_RECIPE step 2) supplies every word, numeral, rule and logo exactly as written in MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY.

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
- Both prices (₹9, ₹99), all labels, tagline, credibility strip, CTA and URL: set as real vector/type, not generated — mandatory for numeral accuracy and legibility.
- Wordmark "aight." reproduced exactly as the brand's own lockup/typeface convention (lowercase, period), taken from the official site, not reinterpreted.
- Grid, column widths, rule-line placement and hairline border geometry: built deterministically to the spec above, not left to model interpretation.

AUDIO_AND_EDIT
Not applicable — static image deliverable.

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- Risk: model renders "sale-like" ornament (bursts, ribbons, percentage-off badges) when it sees "festive + price." Mitigation: background prompt explicitly excludes these, and no pricing/promo language is ever sent to the image model — prices are added deterministically afterward.
- Risk: ₹9 next to ₹99 reads as a joke/mismatch (one price looks like an afterthought). Mitigation: identical cap-height, identical row treatment, separated only by a thin rule, framed as two rows of one table, not as "from ₹9."
- Risk: festive motif drifts into generic diya/marigold clipart look, undercutting premium infra tone. Mitigation: motif restricted to abstract hairline geometry and light glow only; no illustrated festival objects.
- Risk: text illegible at small feed sizes. Mitigation: thumbnail legibility check specified in production recipe; price numerals sized to remain dominant at reduced scale.
- Risk: brand misrepresentation. Mitigation: all brand claims (models offered, wallet, GST invoice, support model) restricted to what getaight.ai actually states; no invented feature claims.

HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
- Single poster only, no alternates: met.
- 4:5 ratio: met.
- Both prices immediately understandable: met via matched hero ledger module, largest type on page.
- Premium AI-infrastructure feel, not discount retail flyer: met via rate-card framing, ledger typography, restrained palette, explicit exclusion of discount iconography.
- Typography/hierarchy does most of the work: met — grid-governed bands, tabular numerals, weight/scale contrast are the sole hierarchy mechanism; imagery is reduced to atmosphere only.
- Only getaight.ai used for brand/product facts: met — tagline, wordmark form, model roster (GPT/Claude/Gemini/curated image), wallet/GST/support claims all sourced from the site snapshot; no other source used for brand facts.
- No model/provider recommended: met.

## PACKAGE B

RESEARCH_NEEDS:
How to build strict typographic/information hierarchy so price is the dominant, immediately-legible element without decorative noise.
How to signal "serious infrastructure" rather than "discount promotion" through pricing framing and visual restraint (India-specific pricing psychology).
How to encode festive/cultural signal legibly and briefly without literal festive clipart, consistent with how Indian visual communication compresses culture into minimal cues.

FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

DELIVERABLE
One static 4:5 poster, master file 2400×3000 px (scalable to 1080×1350 for social), RGB, print-safe margins. Single image, single concept, no variants.

OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
The customer needs a festive-season acquisition poster for an API product whose entire commercial hook is two prices (₹9 image / ₹99 video). The stated risk is that low, round rupee numbers read as "discount flyer" unless the design frames them as engineered, infrastructure-grade pricing. Because aight's own site voice is plain, declarative, and proof-of-seriousness driven (prepaid rupees, one GST invoice, a founder who answers), the poster's credibility must come from the same register: precision, restraint, and a rate-card logic — not festive clipart or retail sale mechanics. Typography and hierarchy must therefore carry both the emotional (festive, premium) and functional (price, comprehension) load, since no product photography exists to lean on.

CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
"The Festive Rate Card." The poster is composed like a utility/telecom tariff card or a GST invoice header — a format Indian businesses already read as official, exact, and non-negotiable — rather than like a sale flyer. Two prices sit in a tabular, equal-weight rate table. Festivity is carried by a single restrained device (a hairline gold rangoli-lattice border and a warm lamp-glow gradient), never by literal diyas, fireworks, or "OFFER/SALE" iconography. The pitch to the viewer is implicit: this isn't a discount, it's what the infrastructure actually costs — echoing the site's own framing of pricing as structural (prepaid, capped, invoiced) rather than promotional.

MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. Price pair (₹9 / ₹99, tabular, largest type on the page) — the single thing every viewer must retain, legible even at thumbnail size, no cultural code required to parse it (numerals travel universally).
2. Unit labels ("IMAGE GENERATION" / "VIDEO GENERATION") — disambiguate the two prices instantly.
3. Trust line ("Prepaid rupees. One GST invoice. No surprise bills.") — converts low price into infrastructure credibility, sourced verbatim in spirit from the site's own proof points.
4. Wordmark + tagline ("aight. — Where Indian businesses buy AI") — brand anchor, deliberately modest in size relative to the price.
5. Festive frame line ("Festive-season infrastructure pricing") — supplies seasonal context without discount language.
6. CTA ("Talk to us — getaight.ai") + fine-print rate disclaimer — lowest priority, smallest type.
Everything below tier 1 is optional to a skimming viewer; nothing above tier 1 is allowed to compete with it in size or contrast.

VISUAL_SYSTEM
Palette: near-black charcoal background, off-white type, single antique/brass-gold accent. No red/yellow "sale" colors, no multicolor festive gradient.
Grid: strict single-column vertical grid, generous margins, center third reserved for the price block, top for wordmark, bottom for CTA/legal — structure emerges from grouping like-with-like information (price data together, trust data together, brand data together), so the layout reads as inevitable rather than decorated.
Typography: one grotesk/neo-grotesque sans family across the whole poster, varying only weight and size; tabular (lining) figures mandatory so ₹9 and ₹99 share a baseline grid like a real rate card; small caps + letterspacing for labels to reinforce "spec sheet" tone.
Motif: a single 1–2pt hairline border built from a repeating rangoli-derived diamond/dot lattice, running the full frame edge — geometric and architectural, not ornamental, so it reads as festive to an Indian viewer while still looking engineered. A soft warm glow in the upper third evokes lamplight without depicting a lamp.
No product photography, no people, no icons of AI (no robots/circuits), since the product is invisible infrastructure — the type system itself must do all persuasive work.

PRODUCTION_RECIPE
1. Generate the text-free background/texture/motif layer (prompt below).
2. Set all copy and numerals as vector type in a design tool on the grid specified above (deterministic, not generated).
3. Composite type over background; apply the gold hairline divider between the two price rows.
4. QA pass at three sizes: full res, 1080px feed, 300px thumbnail — confirm ₹9 and ₹99 remain instantly legible and correctly rendered at all three.
5. Final color-grade/contrast check (off-white text ≥ 4.5:1 contrast against charcoal); export 4:5 master and social crop.

GENERATION_PROMPTS
Single base-layer generation prompt (background only — all text added deterministically afterward):

"A premium, minimal 4:5 vertical poster background for a serious AI infrastructure brand during India's festive season. Near-black charcoal background (#0B0B0C) with a soft, subtle warm dark-amber glow rising from the upper third, evoking diya lamplight without depicting any lamp, flame, or festive object literally. A thin (1–2pt) hairline geometric border in muted antique gold runs along all four edges, built from a small repeating rangoli-derived diamond-and-dot lattice — precise, symmetrical, architectural, not decorative or cartoonish. The central two-thirds of the frame must remain completely empty, clean, evenly lit, and free of any text, numerals, logos, people, product imagery, icons, fireworks, confetti, or clipart — this space is reserved for typography added later. Subtle fine grain / brushed-material micro-texture for tactile quality. Palette strictly limited to charcoal black, off-white, and one antique gold. No gradients beyond a faint soft glow, no lens flare, no bright multicolor festive elements. Mood: an engineering rate card crossed with a quiet festive invitation — expensive, restrained, confident. Output 2400×3000 px, 4:5 aspect ratio, no watermark."

Construction plan for the type layer (deterministic, composited on top — see below).

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
All text is deterministic vector type, never generated as pixels, because price accuracy is a hard constraint and generative text rendering of currency symbols/numerals is unreliable at this stakes level:
- Wordmark: "aight." — off-white, modest size, top-left.
- Tagline: "Where Indian businesses buy AI" — Light weight, 60% opacity, beneath wordmark.
- Price row 1: label "IMAGE GENERATION" (small caps, gold, letterspaced) + numeral "₹9" (Bold/Black, off-white, tabular figures, largest element on page) + suffix "/ generation" (Light, 70% opacity).
- Gold hairline divider rule (same gold as border motif) between the two price rows.
- Price row 2: label "VIDEO GENERATION" + numeral "₹99", identical size/weight/treatment to row 1 so both prices carry equal visual authority.
- Trust line: "Prepaid rupees. One GST invoice. No surprise bills." — off-white, medium weight, centered.
- Festive frame line: "Festive-season infrastructure pricing" — small, gold, no "SALE/OFFER/DISCOUNT/% OFF" wording anywhere on the poster.
- CTA: "Talk to us — getaight.ai" inside a slim outlined pill, off-white.
- Fine print, bottom corner, low opacity: "Introductory festive-season rate. Terms at getaight.ai."
All grid positions, type sizes, tracking values, and color hex codes are locked in production spec before compositing — no randomness permitted in this layer.

AUDIO_AND_EDIT
Not applicable. This is a single static image deliverable; no audio track or edit timeline is required.

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- Currency/numeral misrendering → mitigated entirely by rendering ₹9/₹99 as deterministic vector type, never generated text.
- Drifting into "discount flyer" territory → banned assets: starbursts, ribbons, "%", red/yellow sale colors, countdown/urgency language; enforced by rate-card tabular layout instead.
- Festive motif turning kitsch/stock → motif restricted to one abstract geometric lattice + one glow, no literal diya/firework/confetti imagery, per construction plan.
- Hierarchy collapse from clutter → hard cap of six copy blocks total; if any block competes visually with the price numerals in size or saturation, it is downsized in QA.
- Illegibility at small/thumbnail sizes → mandatory three-size QA pass before export; tabular lining figures and sufficient weight chosen specifically for compression survival.
- Overclaiming/legal exposure on "festive rate" → fine-print disclaimer included by default.

HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
- Exactly one 4:5 poster produced: yes.
- ₹9 (image) and ₹99 (video) both present and immediately understandable: yes — largest, tabular, equal-weight, deterministic type.
- Feels like serious AI infrastructure company, not discount retail: yes — rate-card structure, restrained palette, no sale iconography, trust line drawn from the brand's actual infra proof points.
- Typography and information hierarchy do most of the work: yes — no product photography or illustrative scene; the entire poster is type + one abstract motif.
- Only getaight.ai used for brand/product facts: yes — wordmark, tagline, prepaid-rupee/GST/founder-support claims sourced only from the site snapshot; no other brand assumptions invented.
- No image/video model or provider named or recommended: yes.
- Single concept, no alternatives offered: yes.

## PACKAGE C

FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

DELIVERABLE
One (1) static poster, 4:5 aspect ratio (e.g., 1600×2000 px minimum, scalable to print), for digital + print festive campaign use. Single final composition, no variants.

OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
The customer needs a poster that does two things simultaneously without contradiction: (1) make ₹9 (image) and ₹99 (video) instantly, unambiguously readable as the commercial hook, and (2) read as serious AI infrastructure branding — precise, metered, invoice-grade — not a retail discount flyer. Per aight's own site language ("prepaid rupees," "hard caps," "one domestic GST invoice," "a founder who answers"), the brand's actual differentiator is operational precision and trustworthy plumbing, not cheapness. The poster must translate low price into evidence of engineering efficiency ("this is what infrastructure pricing looks like"), not into evidence of a sale. Festive season is the occasion/context, not the visual language driver — it should be present as a restrained seasonal cue, not a decorative takeover.

CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
"The Rate Card, Engineered." Treat the poster as a precision price ledger / meter reading rather than a promotional badge. Two SKU-style line items — IMAGE and VIDEO — are set with tabular, monospace-style numerals at equal visual weight, aligned on a shared vertical rule like a utility bill or infrastructure dashboard. Festive identity is carried through a single restrained motif (a minimal foil-line diya/rangoli mark used as a typographic divider/ornament, not a scene) and a jewel-tone palette (deep indigo/charcoal ground, warm gold linework, one small vermilion/marigold accent) — festive through material and color discipline, not through clutter or starbursts. Hierarchy is built entirely by grid geometry and type weight (per structural design principle: assign each kind of information — brand, price, proof, CTA — its own column width and weight so the ordering is read off the structure itself, not off decoration).

MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. Brand identity block (smallest zone, top): wordmark "aight." + one-line positioning "Where Indian businesses buy AI." — establishes credibility register before price is seen.
2. Festive ornament divider (thin band): single gold foil-line diya/rangoli mark — signals occasion, does not compete for attention.
3. PRIMARY zone (largest, visual center of gravity, ~45% of vertical space): the two-line price ledger —
   IMAGE GENERATION — ₹9
   VIDEO GENERATION — ₹99
   Set at identical scale/weight to imply parity of the two product lines and precision, not a "starting from" teaser.
4. Proof/trust line (small, directly below ledger): "GPT · Claude · Gemini · Sarvam — one prepaid wallet. One GST invoice."
5. CTA + footer band (bottom, smallest, quiet): "Talk to us" · getaight.ai · one-line reassurance "Prepaid credits. Hard caps. No forex. No reverse charge."
No countdown clocks, no "OFFER," no slashed prices, no percentage-off language — nothing in the discount-flyer register.

VISUAL_SYSTEM
- Palette: near-black indigo/charcoal ground (#0E1015–#12131A range); warm metallic gold linework and primary numerals (#C9A24B–#D4AF37 range); single restrained vermilion/marigold accent (#D9642F–#E2703A) used only on the ornament flame-tip or the shared vertical rule — festive without being bright/retail.
- Typography: a disciplined two-family system — a geometric/grotesk sans for brand, tagline, proof line, CTA (small, controlled tracking); a tabular/monospace-leaning numeral face for the ₹9 / ₹99 figures so they read as metered data, not marketing type. Rupee symbol and numerals given generous scale and letterspacing precision; unit text ("/ generation") set much smaller and lighter beside each number so the number itself is the unmistakable read.
- Grid: strict single-column-width system top to bottom; every information class (brand, ornament, price ledger, proof, footer) occupies its own fixed band and shared left/right margins — no floating badges, no diagonal banners, no starburst shapes. A single thin vertical or horizontal gold rule ties the two price lines together, reinforcing "these are line items of one system," not two separate promos.
- Texture: subtle premium paper/foil-stamp micro-texture on the dark ground; no photographic clutter, no crowded festive iconography (no marketplace bunting, no confetti, no glitter bursts).
- Negative space: generous margins around the price block so it reads as confident and engineered, per the discipline that reduced, well-spaced elements differentiate themselves more effectively than a busy field.

PRODUCTION_RECIPE
Two-layer build to guarantee price/text accuracy (generative models are unreliable at exact small numerals/currency symbols at high fidelity):
Layer 1 — AI-generated background/ornamental plate: dark jewel-tone textured ground with the gold foil-line diya/rangoli ornament, subtle premium texture, and the grid guide bands, with all typography areas left clean/negative-space only (no AI-rendered text).
Layer 2 — Deterministic typesetting overlay: all copy (wordmark, tagline, "IMAGE GENERATION — ₹9," "VIDEO GENERATION — ₹99," proof line, CTA, footer) set as vector type in design software using the specified type system, composited exactly per the grid. This is the layer that must render the prices — never leave ₹9/₹99 to open-ended generative text rendering.
Final export: flatten to single 4:5 image, check contrast/legibility at thumbnail size (mobile feed scale) before finalizing.

GENERATION_PROMPTS
(Image deliverable — one executable final generation prompt for Layer 1, the background/ornamental plate; text is added deterministically per above.)

"Premium minimalist background plate for an Indian festive AI-infrastructure poster, 4:5 vertical composition, 1600x2000px. Deep indigo-charcoal ground (#12131A) with subtle premium foil-stamp micro-texture, like fine matte cardstock under low studio light. Centered upper-third: one small, elegant, restrained gold foil-line ornament combining a minimal diya (oil lamp) silhouette with a geometric rangoli motif, rendered as thin single-weight gold linework (#D4AF37), no fill, no glitter, no bokeh, no confetti — architectural and precise, like an engraved icon, not a festive illustration. A single thin horizontal gold rule spans the middle third of the frame at about 55% height, extending edge-to-edge within margins, to anchor a future price ledger. One small warm vermilion accent (#D9642F) only at the ornament's flame tip, otherwise entirely gold-on-indigo monochrome. Even, soft top-lighting; no harsh gradients, no color noise, no other iconography, no rupee symbols, no text, no numerals, no logos rendered by the model — those are added afterward. Generous open negative space in the top band, center band, and bottom band for typography to be placed later. Mood: engineered, quiet, expensive, trustworthy — a fintech/infrastructure aesthetic borrowing a single festive motif, not a festive/retail aesthetic. No people, no product mockups, no UI screenshots, no photographic elements."

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
- All text strings (wordmark "aight.", tagline, "IMAGE GENERATION — ₹9", "VIDEO GENERATION — ₹99", "/ generation" unit labels, proof line, CTA "Talk to us", URL "getaight.ai", footer reassurance line) — set as vector type, not generated.
- Exact hex palette and grid margins as specified above — locked in layout software.
- The shared vertical/horizontal rule tying the two price lines — drawn as a precise vector line, not AI-rendered.
- Legal/registration footer text if required by brand (e.g., entity name) is optional and can be added in a minimum 6–7pt line if compliance requires it; not otherwise part of the primary composition.

AUDIO_AND_EDIT
Not applicable — static poster deliverable, no audio/motion/edit component.

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- Risk: generative model renders incorrect/garbled currency figures → mitigated by keeping all numerals and currency symbols in the deterministic typesetting layer only.
- Risk: festive iconography overwhelms the price message, tipping into "discount flyer" territory → mitigated by restricting festive cues to one restrained gold-line ornament and jewel-tone palette, with the grid enforcing equal, generous negative space around the price ledger.
- Risk: two different prices for two different products confuse hierarchy (which is the "headline" price?) → mitigated by giving IMAGE and VIDEO equal type weight/scale in the same ledger format, explicitly framing them as two SKU line items of one system rather than one hero price with a smaller second price.
- Risk: poster reads as cheap because price is low → mitigated by pricing being presented as a precise, metered ledger entry (tabular numerals, ledger rule, invoice-adjacent language "one GST invoice") rather than a slashed/highlighted discount, per the structural insight that low price signals value-engineering rather than desperation when framed as supplier precision, not consumer bargain-hunting.
- Risk: illegibility at small (mobile feed) sizes → mitigated by testing the price block's scale and contrast against the primary hierarchy requirement before final export; unit labels kept secondary in size so the numerals alone are legible at thumbnail scale.

HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
- Exactly one poster, 4:5 — satisfied, no alternates presented.
- Both prices (₹9 image, ₹99 video) immediately understandable — satisfied via equal-weight ledger format as the visual center of gravity.
- Must feel like serious AI infrastructure/product company, not discount retail flyer — satisfied via ledger/rate-card metaphor, restrained festive motif, jewel-tone palette, absence of discount-flyer devices (no starbursts, slashed prices, countdown, percentage-off).
- Typography/information hierarchy carries the work — satisfied; entire layout is grid- and weight-driven with no reliance on illustrative scene-setting.
- Only getaight.ai used for brand/product facts — satisfied; no other source used for product claims.
- No model/provider named or recommended — satisfied.

## JUDGE IMG10
Apply the Stage-1 image rubric. Choose exactly one A/B/C winner.

# CONTEST IMG11 — B03

Use frozen brief B03 from the top of the final file.

## PACKAGE A

FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

DELIVERABLE
One (1) premium advertising still image, 4:5 vertical (deliver at 4000×5000 px, RGB, 300ppi print-safe / social-safe), for a new Indian mosambi (sweet lime) + sparkling water beverage. Single hero-product composition. No celebrity, no external location/website branding, no logotype of any existing company.

OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
The brief asks for a beverage image that reads as premium and contemporary to a 22–35 urban Indian audience while keeping the fruit (mosambi) recognisable and emotionally familiar — without using the usual "Indianness" shorthand (flag colours, paisley, diyas, marigolds, Taj Mahal silhouettes, Devanagari flourishes) and without the visual grammar of a mass-market fizzy soft drink (neon colour, cartoon condensation, cluttered can graphics, harsh top-light). The task is therefore a materiality and restraint problem: signal "premium sparkling citrus" through glass, light, colour and fruit realism rather than through cultural iconography or soda-category tropes, and keep the product itself as the unambiguous focal subject.

CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
"Modern Citrus Clarity" — the bottle is treated as a small glass object of quality (closer to a fine tonic/craft-soda or premium water aesthetic than a soda-fountain drink). Mosambi's familiarity is carried entirely through colour (pale, sun-washed chartreuse-gold), a single freshly cut mosambi half/twist, and visible natural pulp/texture in the liquid — never through text, ethnic props, or literal "Indian" scene-setting. Sophistication comes from restrained studio lighting, a soft tonal gradient backdrop, and jewel-like carbonation detail (fine bubbles, a controlled droplet field, a frozen micro-splash), positioning the product as contemporary craft rather than commodity soda.

MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. Product bottle — largest, sharpest, highest-contrast element, dead-centre vertical axis (unmistakable hero, per the classic "make the product the hero" print-advertising principle).
2. Mosambi cue — single fresh-cut fruit element (half + twist) at the base/side, colour-matched to the liquid, confirming flavour instantly without a fruit pile or crate cliché.
3. Carbonation/refreshment cue — rising bubble trail inside the liquid + light condensation on glass + one restrained arrested droplet/mist detail, communicating "sparkling" and "cold" without a splashy soda-ad explosion.
4. Brand mark — small, minimal wordmark/lockup in the upper third negative space, Latin-script only, no supporting clutter.
5. Optional micro-tagline (e.g., a two-to-four-word line) — smallest element, bottom third, only if brand supplies copy; otherwise omit rather than force text.

VISUAL_SYSTEM
- Palette: pale chartreuse-gold liquid; warm ivory/soft stone background gradient (not white studio, not saturated soda-green); one metallic accent (brushed brass/warm gold cap or foil detail) to signal premium; no red/orange/tricolour soda cues.
- Container: slim, tall glass bottle (not a can, not moulded PET) with a minimal debossed or single-colour label — glass chosen specifically to read "premium" rather than "mass beverage."
- Light: soft, large top-rear key to build a clean vertical specular highlight down the bottle's curve; low-angle bounce fill at front to hold shadow detail; background lit separately on a gentle gradient so the bottle silhouette separates cleanly. Key raised high enough, and a small gobo positioned at the label's reflection angle, so the label stays legible and free of blown specular glare while the glass still gets its describing highlight (a standard product-photography glass/label conflict resolution).
- Composition: vertical thirds; bottle occupies the central third at ~60–65% of frame height; generous negative space above for the brand lockup and below for the fruit garnish and a soft reflection/shadow anchor; background kept clean with only a very soft, out-of-focus suggestion of citrus-grove greenery (barely legible, purely atmospheric — not a literal orchard scene) to avoid both blankness and cliché.
- Texture: photographic realism — real-glass refraction, true condensation bead behaviour, natural pulp flecks in the liquid, matte-not-glossy label finish.
- Typography direction (for the deterministic overlay, not generated): clean modern sans or a contemporary serif, tracked out, lower-case or small-caps, no ornamental Indian script styling — contemporary Indian design increasingly signals identity through material and colour rather than through overt script/text ornament, which supports this restraint.

PRODUCTION_RECIPE
- Framing: 4:5 vertical, product-macro perspective (equivalent ~90–100mm macro look), camera at bottle mid-height, very slight downward tilt (~5°) to show liquid clarity and the fruit garnish plane.
- Depth of field: moderate-shallow (bottle fully sharp front-to-back label and cap; background falls to soft bokeh within ~1 bottle-width behind it).
- Lighting rig: large soft key above and slightly behind subject for the vertical glass highlight; secondary soft fill camera-left/front-low to open shadow side of bottle without flattening it; small negative-fill or flag camera-right to preserve one deep edge for dimensionality (bright side / shadow side / mid side, not a fully lit flat wrap); background gradient lit independently, warm ivory to pale stone, no hot spots.
- Styling: bottle chilled to natural, not artificially heavy, condensation — light bead pattern concentrated lower third, one or two larger runoff beads, not uniform "spray" coating; one clean mosambi half with visible pulp and a thin peel twist resting against/near the base; a small number of natural-looking bubbles rising inside the liquid; optional single arrested droplet or micro-splash accent near the cap, not a violent splash crown.
- Surface: matte pale stone or brushed concrete plinth (very subtle, out of focus) to avoid a "coaster on a kitchen counter" read and avoid an overtly rustic/ethnic surface choice.
- Reflection: soft, short, controlled tabletop reflection under the bottle for premium studio polish.

GENERATION_PROMPTS
Single final generation prompt (image):

"Premium advertising product photograph, 4:5 vertical composition, a single slim tall glass bottle of pale chartreuse-gold sparkling citrus beverage standing centered on a softly lit warm ivory-to-stone gradient background. Photorealistic glass refraction, fine natural condensation beads concentrated on the lower half of the bottle, a few natural rising carbonation bubbles visible inside the pale liquid, a minimal unlabelled or single-tone matte label area left blank for text, a small brushed-brass or matte gold bottle cap. Beside the base, one freshly cut mosambi (sweet lime) half showing visible pulp and juice sheen, plus a thin curled peel twist, softly resting on a pale matte stone surface. Soft large top-rear key light creating one clean vertical specular highlight along the bottle's curve, gentle front-low fill opening the shadow side, one edge kept slightly darker for dimensional roundness, no harsh hotspots on the label area. Background gradient clean and airy, with only a barely-visible, extremely soft out-of-focus suggestion of green citrus-leaf bokeh at the very top edge, kept subtle and non-literal. Short soft reflection of the bottle on the stone surface. Overall mood: sophisticated, contemporary, minimal, refreshing, high-end still-life beverage photography — not a soda advertisement. Studio product photography, ultra-sharp on bottle and fruit, soft shallow falloff on background, natural colour grading, no text, no logos, no additional props, no people, no saturated primary colours, no traditional Indian iconography (no paisley, no marigold, no diya, no tricolour, no Devanagari script), no plastic bottle, no can, no splashing crown or violent liquid explosion, no cartoon-style bubbles."

Negative/exclusion terms to enforce: celebrity/human figures, tricolour or flag palette, paisley/ethnic pattern, diya/marigold/festival props, Taj Mahal or monument silhouettes, Devanagari lettering, neon soda colours, plastic PET bottle, aluminium can, cluttered label design, cartoonish splash, visible brand names of real products.

CONSTRUCTION_PLAN (post-generation, single-image workflow):
1. Generate base hero image per the prompt above at final 4:5 resolution.
2. Colour-grade pass: confirm liquid reads as pale citrus-gold (not neon lime, not orange-soda), background stays warm-neutral, one metallic accent present.
3. Retouch pass: clean up any label artefacting/text-garbling from generation; if the model rendered illegible pseudo-text on the label, remove/smooth it to a blank matte field.
4. Deterministic overlay pass (see next section): place brand wordmark/lockup and optional tagline as vector type in the pre-defined upper-third negative space; place any mandatory regulatory text (net quantity, FSSAI logo placeholder, etc.) in small print at the bottom margin per packaging-law convention — added as clean vector type, not generated.
5. Final polish: verify product remains the single largest, sharpest, most contrasted element in the frame (hero check) before lock.

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
- Brand wordmark/logotype and tagline: created and placed as vector type in post-production, not left to the generative model, since generative text is unreliable and brand identity must be pixel-precise.
- Any mandatory regulatory/label copy (net volume, FSSAI-style mark, ingredient callouts): added as deterministic vector overlay in the final compositing step, positioned in the bottom margin, kept small and out of the primary sightline.
- Final colour calibration/grading pass: done deterministically in post to lock exact palette values (liquid hue, background gradient stops, metallic accent tone) rather than trusting generation variance.

AUDIO_AND_EDIT
Not applicable — deliverable is a single static image with no audio or edit timeline.

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- Risk: generated label text becomes garbled pseudo-script → Mitigation: prompt for a blank/minimal label field; add all real type in post as deterministic vector overlay.
- Risk: image drifts toward mass soft-drink visual grammar (neon colour, cartoon splash, can shape) → Mitigation: explicit negative prompts against can/plastic/neon/cartoon splash; palette locked to pale chartreuse-gold + ivory + brass; container specified as glass.
- Risk: "Indian" cues rendered as literal ethnic iconography → Mitigation: explicit exclusion list (paisley, diya, marigold, tricolour, Devanagari, monuments); cultural identity carried only through the mosambi fruit itself and warm/earthy tonal choice, not through symbolic props — avoiding the common category habit of grafting generic "heritage" signifiers onto packaging for differentiation.
- Risk: product loses hero status to background or garnish → Mitigation: composition rule enforced (bottle largest/sharpest/highest-contrast, centred, background and fruit strictly secondary in scale and focus).
- Risk: glass highlight vs. label legibility conflict (light needed for the glass sheen also glares the label) → Mitigation: key light raised/angled and a gobo used at the label's reflection angle so the glass keeps its describing highlight while the label plane stays clean, per standard glass-product lighting practice.

HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
- Format 4:5, single image: satisfied.
- Indian mosambi + sparkling water beverage, urban 22–35 audience: satisfied via product concept, palette and styling.
- Refreshing, sophisticated, culturally contemporary, retains mosambi familiarity: satisfied via fruit garnish/colour cue + restrained premium styling.
- Avoid clichéd "Indian" visual shorthand: satisfied via explicit exclusion list and reliance on material/colour rather than iconography.
- Avoid cheap-soft-drink look: satisfied via glass bottle, muted palette, controlled condensation/bubbles, premium lighting.
- Product must be unmistakable hero: satisfied via composition hierarchy and scale/focus/contrast rules.
- No celebrity: satisfied — no human figures in image.
- No external website: satisfied — no site, URL, or third-party brand referenced.
- Single concept only, no alternatives offered: satisfied.

## PACKAGE B

FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

DELIVERABLE
One (1) premium advertising still image, 4:5 vertical (recommended output 1080×1350 px, scalable to print resolution), for a new Indian mosambi + sparkling water beverage. Single final composited image, no variants.

OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
The customer needs a hero product image that can carry paid social/OOH-style advertising for a new premium sparkling beverage. The brief's real creative problem is a positioning tightrope: the drink must look expensive, modern and credible to an urban 22–35 audience (who default-associate "Indian soft drink" with cheap, syrupy, primary-colour carbonated products), while still being legible as mosambi-based rather than a generic "citrus fizz" — and it must do this without leaning on the stock ethnic-signifier vocabulary (marigolds, henna, brass thalis, saris, diyas, Rajasthani textile, Taj Mahal silhouettes, spice piles) that "Indian premium" ads over-rely on. The fruit itself — its actual botanical identity, not a cultural symbol — is the one culturally-familiar element allowed to carry weight.

CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
"The fruit, elevated." A single, unmistakably premium glass bottle of the drink is shot as a quiet, editorial still-life object — the kind of restrained, material-led photography used for artisanal spirits or fine mixers — with real mosambi (sweet lime, pale green-gold skin, distinct from lemon/orange) cut and beaded with juice beside it as the only cultural anchor. No ethnic props, no festival colour, no crowd or lifestyle scene. Sophistication comes from material honesty (matte glass, natural stone, controlled light, muted warm palette) rather than decoration.

MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. Product identity (bottle + liquid colour + fruit) — dominant, ~65–70% of visual weight, sharp focus, largest and tallest element, brightest specular highlight in the frame.
2. Fruit/flavour proof (halved mosambi, peel curl, droplets) — secondary, smaller scale, slightly softer focus, physically touching/orbiting the bottle so the eye reads "made from this."
3. Brand wordmark + one-line descriptor — tertiary, minimal type, placed in the reserved negative-space band at top (wordmark) and bottom (descriptor/volume), added as a clean deterministic overlay, not part of the generated scene.
4. Atmosphere (condensation, light, background material) — supporting only; must never compete with 1 or 2.

VISUAL_SYSTEM
- Format: 4:5 vertical, product occupies the central-to-lower-middle vertical axis, rule-of-thirds placement slightly left or right of centre, generous negative space top and bottom reserved for type (per compositional principle that negative space is a designed shape, not leftover area).
- Palette: warm neutrals — bone, warm plaster/terracotta, soft sage, brushed brass — with the drink's own pale chartreuse-gold as the single accent colour. No neon green, no cyan, no rainbow gradient, no glossy plastic sheen.
- Bottle: slim 250ml glass bottle, matte or lightly frosted finish, crown cap or brushed-metal cap, minimal debossed/foil-adjacent label area (kept visually clean/blank-textured in the generated plate — see Deterministic section).
- Liquid: pale, slightly cloudy mosambi-gold, visible fine carbonation bead trail, not neon yellow (avoid reading as "lemonade").
- Props: 1 whole mosambi, 1 halved mosambi showing segments and juice beads, one thin peel twist, a few loose condensation droplets/scattered water beads on the stone surface. Nothing else.
- Background: warm, textured, minimal — a single plane of natural material (limewashed plaster, honed stone, or raw linen) lit by soft, large, directional daylight-style light from one side, gentle falloff to shadow at the edges. No pattern, no busy set-dressing.
- Lighting: large soft key camera-left or camera-right (soft-box/window quality), gentle fill, a controlled large-but-dimmed highlight along the bottle's glass body (a big soft highlight reads as glass surface information; keep it dim, not a small blown hot spot) plus a crisper small highlight on the cap for material contrast.
- Camera: eye-level to very slightly above, macro-adjacent product-photography lens character (85–100mm equivalent), shallow-to-moderate depth of field — bottle fully sharp, background and foremost fruit slightly softened to separate planes.

PRODUCTION_RECIPE
Step 1 — Generate the photographic plate (bottle, fruit, surface, background, light, condensation) with the label area left clean/blank-textured, no legible text baked in.
Step 2 — Colour/contrast pass to lock the warm-neutral premium palette and confirm the liquid colour reads as mosambi, not lemon or lime.
Step 3 — Deterministic compositing pass: place real vector wordmark, tagline, volume/regulatory microcopy, and label graphic onto the bottle and into the reserved top/bottom negative space using actual brand typography assets (not generated text).
Step 4 — Final grade and 4:5 crop lock; check product remains the single largest, sharpest, brightest element.

GENERATION_PROMPTS
Single image — one executable final generation prompt (photographic plate, pre-type-compositing):

"Premium editorial product photograph, vertical 4:5 composition. A single slim 250ml matte-frosted glass bottle of pale, subtly cloudy chartreuse-gold sparkling beverage stands slightly off-centre on a honed warm-stone surface, fine carbonation bubbles visible rising inside the liquid, cap in brushed brass or matte metal, label area on the bottle left clean and softly textured with no legible text or logo. Beside the bottle: one whole pale-green-skinned mosambi (Indian sweet lime, botanically distinct from lemon or orange), one mosambi cut in half showing pale yellow-green segments and a few beads of fresh juice, and a single thin curl of mosambi peel. Fine condensation droplets bead on the bottle's glass surface and scatter lightly on the stone surface nearby. Background is a single plane of warm limewashed plaster or raw linen in muted bone/terracotta tones, softly out of focus, lit by one large soft directional daylight-quality light source from the side, gentle shadow falloff at the frame edges, one large but dimmed soft highlight running down the glass body of the bottle plus one smaller crisper highlight on the cap. Shallow-to-moderate depth of field: bottle and cut fruit tack-sharp, background and farthest props gently softened. Muted, warm, sophisticated colour palette — no neon colours, no bright primary red/blue/green, no glossy plastic look, no splash or spray effects, no cartoon citrus burst, no confetti or festive colour. Negative space reserved at the top 12% and bottom 15% of frame, kept clean and uncluttered for later typography. Mood: quiet, confident, tactile, minimal, expensive — closer to an artisanal spirits or fine-mixer advertisement than a soft-drink ad. Shot on a product-photography macro-capable lens, eye-level, soft studio-quality light. No text, no logos, no watermark, no people, no hands, no marigolds, no henna, no diyas, no rangoli, no sari or turban fabric, no brass religious objects, no spice piles, no Taj Mahal or monument imagery, no elephants, no bindis, no festival colour powder, no plastic bottle, no can, no straw, no ice cubes, no splash droplet crown, no rainbow gradient background."

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
- Brand wordmark, tagline/descriptor line ("Sparkling Mosambi" or brand-supplied equivalent), volume, and any regulatory/legal microcopy: rendered as vector typography and composited in post — not generated inside the image model, to guarantee crisp, on-brand, legible type (generative text rendering is unreliable and would undermine the "premium" read).
- Bottle label graphic/final logo lockup: applied as a deterministic overlay onto the pre-left-blank label area identified in the prompt.
- Any regulatory FSSAI/nutrition mark or barcode required for the market: added as deterministic post-production elements, sized to remain visually subordinate to the hero product.

AUDIO_AND_EDIT
Not applicable — static single advertising image, no audio or motion edit required.

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- Risk: liquid or bottle reads as generic lemon-lime soda → mitigated by explicit mosambi fruit reference, pale chartreuse-gold (not neon yellow) colour direction, and negative prompt against soda-splash/rainbow-gradient tropes.
- Risk: image defaults to clichéd "Indian" prop vocabulary → mitigated by explicit, itemised negative list (marigolds, henna, diyas, rangoli, sari/turban, brass religious objects, monuments, elephants, festival colour) and by restricting cultural signalling to the fruit alone.
- Risk: garbled or fake-looking generated text on label/bottle → mitigated by leaving label area blank/textured in the generation prompt and compositing real typography deterministically afterward.
- Risk: product not read as unmistakable hero → mitigated by explicit compositional dominance instructions (largest, sharpest, brightest element; fruit and props subordinate and slightly softer focus).
- Risk: cheap soft-drink look via glossy plastic/oversaturated colour → mitigated by matte glass, muted warm-neutral palette, and explicit "no plastic bottle / no can / no neon colour" exclusions.

HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
- 4:5 advertising image: met.
- Urban 22–35 sophistication + refreshing + culturally contemporary: met via editorial minimal styling and fruit-led (not symbol-led) cultural cue.
- Retains familiarity of mosambi: met via visible, correctly identified mosambi fruit (not generic citrus).
- Avoids clichéd "Indian" visual shorthand: met via explicit exclusion list.
- Avoids cheap soft-drink look: met via glass bottle, matte finishes, muted palette, no splash/plastic tropes.
- Product is unmistakable hero: met via compositional and lighting dominance rules.
- No celebrity: none included.
- No external website: none referenced or used.

## PACKAGE C

FINAL_PRODUCTION_PACKAGE

DELIVERABLE
One static advertising image, 4:5 vertical (recommend 1080×1350 px minimum, deliverable at print-safe resolution ≥3000×3750 px), single hero product shot. No video, no celebrity, no external website reference.

OBJECTIVE_INTERPRETATION
The customer needs a launch hero image for a new Indian mosambi-sparkling-water drink targeted at urban 22–35 year-olds. The image must do three things simultaneously: (1) make the bottle the unmistakable visual subject, (2) read as premium/editorial rather than mass-market soda, and (3) feel "culturally contemporary" — recognisably rooted in mosambi without leaning on stock Indian iconography (no marigolds, saris, henna, elephants, Taj Mahal, filmy colour grading). The win condition is a shopper/scroller instantly identifying (a) what it is, (b) that it's premium, (c) that it's citrus-fresh and familiar, in under a second, with the product legible even at small thumbnail size.

CORE_CREATIVE_IDEA
A single slender glass bottle, mid-condensation, shot in soft editorial daylight against a warm, minimal, matte backdrop (terracotta/clay tone), with real mosambi (sweet lime) — a halved fruit and a couple of wedges — as the only supporting props, plus visible fine carbonation inside the liquid. Sophistication comes from restraint: one product, one fruit, one accent colour, generous negative space, quiet premium typography — not from cultural motifs or busy soda-commercial energy. The fruit does the "familiarity" work; the styling, light and materials do the "sophistication and contemporary" work.

MESSAGE_AND_INFORMATION_HIERARCHY
1. Bottle (hero, tack-sharp, center-weighted, largest single mass in frame) — communicates category + premium cue instantly.
2. Mosambi fruit (halved + wedge, adjacent, softly out of primary focal plane) — communicates flavour/familiarity, answers "what is this" in one glance.
3. Condensation + visible bubbles — communicates "sparkling," "cold," "refreshing" without any text.
4. Brand wordmark + small abstracted citrus-slice mark on label (part of the product itself, not a floating graphic) — identity carried by the product, per the make-the-product-the-hero principle rather than by a separate graphic device.
5. Optional minimal tagline in bottom-third clear space (e.g., "Mosambi. Sparkling. Elevated.") — smallest, lowest priority, easily dropped if it competes with the bottle.
No other claims, badges, bursts, or percentage callouts — those read as cheap soft-drink shorthand and are deliberately excluded.

VISUAL_SYSTEM
- Palette: warm terracotta/clay background, muted sage-green accent (leaf), brass/copper bottle cap, translucent pale citrus-gold liquid. No neon greens, no primary-blue soda cues.
- Materials: matte stone/clay surface + glass + brass — a tactile, non-plastic material story that reads premium.
- Light: single large soft key (softbox or bounced daylight) positioned high and to one side, sized roughly 10–20× the bottle diameter, angled so its reflection on the glass produces one large, dimmed highlight rather than a small hot specular spot. A large-but-dim highlight reads as considered surface/texture; a small bright highlight reads as cheap and distracting — this governs the entire lighting decision.
- Background treatment for the glass edge: since a round glass/liquid subject acts as a lens and sees a wider field than the camera, the backdrop must be large enough (or the camera positioned close enough) to fill what the liquid itself reflects, otherwise a dark ring or a stray hot rectangle will appear inside the bottle — construction plan below controls for this.
- Composition: bottle placed on a lower-third power point, vertical emphasis suits 4:5; ample clear space above/beside for label legibility and optional tagline; camera near eye-level with a very slight downward tilt to show the liquid's surface/bubbles.
- No clichéd Indian iconography anywhere in frame. Cultural specificity is carried entirely by the real fruit and by a quiet, non-stereotyped colour/material palette — this deliberately trades "instant ethnic legibility" for premium universality, a known trade-off (heavy cultural signage reads as more "authentic" but also as more generic/dated shorthand; a fruit-led, material-led approach reads as premium and still unambiguously mosambi).

PRODUCTION_RECIPE
1. Hero plate: bottle centered slightly left of vertical axis, camera at ~eye level, macro-capable framing, shallow-to-medium depth of field (bottle fully sharp, background falls off softly).
2. Liquid/carbonation: capture or render fine, evenly distributed bubbles rising, pale gold-green liquid, filled to a level that shows both liquid and a visible pour/fizz cue near the neck.
3. Condensation: fine, realistic water beading concentrated on the lower two-thirds of the bottle, a few controlled rivulets — not an all-over frosted look (frosted-all-over reads as cheap/canned soda).
4. Props: one mosambi half (cut face toward camera, pith and segments visible) resting near the base, one or two wedges leaning on the bottle; optionally a single small mint or curry-leaf sprig for a fresh green accent — kept minimal, not decorative clutter.
5. Backdrop: warm terracotta seamless or textured clay surface, large enough in frame (or camera close enough) to fill the field of view the liquid itself reflects, avoiding a visible dark ring or hot studio reflection inside the glass.
6. Label/wordmark: designed and finalized as a deterministic vector asset (see below), composited onto the bottle with correct wrap/perspective — not left to the generative pass to invent.

GENERATION_PROMPTS
Single final image-generation prompt (base plate, product/scene only — label to be composited deterministically afterward):

"Premium editorial product photograph, 4:5 vertical composition. A single slender clear glass bottle of pale gold-green sparkling citrus drink, centered slightly left of frame, fine carbonation bubbles visible rising inside the liquid, condensation droplets beading on the lower two-thirds of the glass with one or two controlled rivulets. Brass-copper metal cap. Bottle stands on a warm matte terracotta clay surface. Beside it, one halved mosambi (sweet lime) showing its cut segments and pith, plus one mosambi wedge leaning against the bottle base, and a single small fresh green leaf sprig for accent. Soft, large, high-angled daylight-style key light from upper left producing one large soft dimmed highlight along the glass, gentle fill, warm neutral tones throughout — terracotta, brass, pale citrus gold, muted sage green. Shallow depth of field, bottle in crisp sharp focus, background softly defocused with no clutter. No text, no logo, no people, no cultural props or ornament, no bright primary colors, no plastic or neon soda aesthetic. Clean generous negative space above and to the right of the bottle for later graphic overlay. Photoreal, high-end beverage advertising style, natural material textures, matte finish, restrained and sophisticated mood."

Construction plan:
- Layer 1 (background): terracotta backdrop, out-of-focus, large enough to avoid reflection artifacts in the glass/liquid.
- Layer 2 (hero): bottle with liquid/carbonation/condensation, sharp focus, centered lower-third.
- Layer 3 (props): mosambi half + wedge + leaf, arranged not to overlap or obscure the bottle's label zone.
- Layer 4 (deterministic overlay, post-generation): label graphic, brand wordmark, small abstracted citrus-slice mark, optional tagline — vector-precise, composited and perspective-matched to the bottle in a compositing/retouch pass.
- Layer 5 (finishing): color grade to lock warm/neutral palette, dial down any specular hot spots that emerged too small/bright, confirm bubble/condensation realism, confirm product retains full legibility at thumbnail scale.

DETERMINISTIC_OR_NON_GENERATIVE_ELEMENTS
- Final label design, wordmark, logotype/mark, and any on-pack legal or tagline text: produced as a fixed vector/graphic asset by a designer and composited onto the bottle in post — not generated freeform, to guarantee spelling, kerning and brand-mark accuracy.
- Brand color values (terracotta, brass, sage) locked to exact hex/Pantone references from brand guidelines, applied in the grading/compositing pass.
- Any nutritional/regulatory text (if required for the market) added as deterministic type in post, not generated.

AUDIO_AND_EDIT
Not applicable — this is a single static image deliverable with no audio or edit timeline.

FAILURE_PREVENTION
- Reject any output where the bottle is not the largest, sharpest, most central mass in frame — hierarchy is non-negotiable.
- Reject small, bright, hard-edged specular highlights on the glass (reads as cheap/soda); require one large, dimmer highlight area instead.
- Reject an all-over "frosted" condensation look; keep it localized and realistic.
- Reject any generated Indian cultural iconography (textiles, marigolds, elephants, monuments, festival colour palettes) — flag and remove if it appears.
- Reject any generated on-bottle text/logo attempt from the model itself; always replace with the deterministic label asset.
- Reject oversaturated, neon, or "soft-drink commercial" color grading; hold to the warm/matte palette.
- Check the glass/liquid area for a visible dark ring or a hot rectangular studio reflection; if present, treat the background/camera framing rather than trying to relight the highlight.
- Verify legibility and hero clarity at reduced (mobile thumbnail) size before final sign-off.

HARD_CONSTRAINT_CHECK
- 4:5 advertising image: satisfied.
- Product is unmistakable hero: satisfied via composition, scale, and focus hierarchy.
- No celebrity: satisfied — no human figures at all.
- No external website: satisfied — no URLs or web references anywhere in the layout.
- Avoid clichéd "Indian" visual shorthand: satisfied — no stereotypical props/iconography; cultural cue carried only by real mosambi fruit.
- Avoid cheap soft-drink look: satisfied via large-soft-highlight lighting discipline, matte materials, restrained palette, no bursts/badges/neon.
- Retain familiarity of mosambi while feeling contemporary/sophisticated: satisfied via real fruit prop + editorial styling/material palette.

Apply the Stage-1 image rubric. Choose exactly one A/B/C winner.
